**Tool Result:** `toolu_vrtx_012cc6V9vE4BU83DhfaF8hKG`

```
The syntax highlighting system in `token-editor` uses **tree-sitter** for background parsing and incremental updates. It follows an Elm-like architecture where document edits trigger debounced parse commands, which are then handled by a worker thread.

### Core Logic & State
- **[src/syntax/parser.rs#L1-L150](file://~/code/token-editor/src/syntax/parser.rs#L1-L150)**: The heart of the syntax system. Manages `tree-sitter` parsers, language initialization, and **incremental parsing** logic.
    - `compute_incremental_edit` (L55-L91) calculates the diff between old and new source to update the syntax tree efficiently.
    - `ParserState` (L129-L190) caches trees and queries per document.
- **[src/syntax/highlights.rs#L1-L148](file://~/code/token-editor/src/syntax/highlights.rs#L1-L148)**: Defines the data structures for highlights (`HighlightToken`, `LineHighlights`, `SyntaxHighlights`) and the mapping from tree-sitter capture names to internal IDs.
- **[src/syntax/languages.rs#L1-L170](file://~/code/token-editor/src/syntax/languages.rs#L1-L170)**: Handles language detection from file extensions and paths, supporting a wide range of languages (Rust, YAML, Markdown, etc.).

### Update & Re-highlighting Logic
- **[src/update/syntax.rs#L16-L219](file://~/code/token-editor/src/update/syntax.rs#L16-L219)**: Orchestrates the highlighting lifecycle.
    - `update_syntax` (L16-L185) handles `ParseReady` (starts background job) and `ParseCompleted` (applies highlights).
    - `schedule_syntax_parse` (L191-L219) implements the **30ms debounce** logic (L13) to avoid re-parsing on every keystroke.
- **[src/messages.rs#L380-L400](file://~/code/token-editor/src/messages.rs#L380-L400)**: Message definitions for syntax events (`ParseReady`, `ParseCompleted`, `LanguageChanged`).

### Integration & Rendering
- **[src/model/document.rs#L63-L141](file://~/code/token-editor/src/model/document.rs#L63-L141)**: Stores the `SyntaxHighlights` and `revision` counter in the document model for staleness checking.
- **[src/view/frame.rs#L458-L495](file://~/code/token-editor/src/view/frame.rs#L458-L495)**: `draw_with_highlights` applies the theme colors to text based on the extracted `HighlightToken` spans.
- **[src/view/mod.rs#L442-L460](file://~/code/token-editor/src/view/mod.rs#L442-L460)**: Retrieves line-specific highlights from the document for rendering.

### Grammars & Queries
- **[queries/](file://~/code/token-editor/queries/)**: Contains `.scm` query files for various languages (e.g., [queries/rust/highlights.scm](file://~/code/token-editor/queries/rust/highlights.scm)).
```

model.editor_area.documents.get_mut(&doc_id).unwrap();\n368:             doc.language = LanguageId::Rust;\n369:             doc.revision = 10;\n370:         }\n371: \n372:         // Send completed results for old revision 5\n373:         let highlights = SyntaxHighlights::new(LanguageId::Rust, 5);\n374:         let cmd = update_syntax(\n375:             &mut model,\n376:             SyntaxMsg::ParseCompleted {\n377:                 document_id: doc_id,\n378:                 revision: 5,\n379:                 highlights,\n380:             },\n381:         );\n382: \n383:         // Should produce no command (stale)\n384:         assert!(cmd.is_none(), \"Stale results should be discarded\");\n385: \n386:         // Highlights should NOT be stored\n387:         let doc = model.editor_area.documents.get(&doc_id).unwrap();\n388:         assert!(doc.syntax_highlights.is_none());\n389:     }\n390: \n391:     #[test]\n392:     fn test_full_syntax_update_flow() {\n393:         // Simulate the complete flow: edit -> schedule -> ready -> parse -> completed\n394:         let mut model = AppModel::new(800, 600, 1.0, vec![]);\n395:         let doc_id = model.document().id.expect(\"Document should have an ID\");\n396: \n397:         // Set up a JavaScript document\n398:         {\n399:             let doc = model.editor_area.documents.get_mut(&doc_id).unwrap();\n400:             doc.language = LanguageId::JavaScript;\n401:             doc.buffer = ropey::Rope::from(\"let x = 1;\");\n402:             doc.revision = 1;\n403:         }\n404: \n405:         // Step 1: Schedule a parse (this would be called after an edit)\n406:         let schedule_cmd = schedule_syntax_parse(&mut model, doc_id);\n407:         assert!(schedule_cmd.is_some());\n408: \n409:         // Step 2: ParseReady comes in (after debounce)\n410:         let run_cmd = update_syntax(\n411:             &mut model,\n412:             SyntaxMsg::ParseReady {\n413:                 document_id: doc_id,\n414:                 revision: 1,\n415:             },\n416:         );\n417:         assert!(matches!(run_cmd, Some(Cmd::RunSyntaxParse { .. })));\n418: \n419:         // Step 3: Simulate actual parsing\n420:         let mut parser_state = ParserState::new();\n421:         let source = model.document().buffer.to_string();\n422:         let highlights =\n423:             parser_state.parse_and_highlight(&source, LanguageId::JavaScript, doc_id, 1);\n424: \n425:         // Step 4: ParseCompleted comes back\n426:         let redraw_cmd = update_syntax(\n427:             &mut model,\n428:             SyntaxMsg::ParseCompleted {\n429:                 document_id: doc_id,\n430:                 revision: 1,\n431:                 highlights,\n432:             },\n433:         );\n434:         assert!(redraw_cmd.is_some());\n435:         assert!(redraw_cmd.as_ref().unwrap().needs_redraw());\n436: \n437:         // Verify highlights are stored\n438:         let doc = model.editor_area.documents.get(&doc_id).unwrap();\n439:         assert!(doc.syntax_highlights.is_some());\n440:         assert!(!doc.syntax_highlights.as_ref().unwrap().lines.is_empty());\n441:     }\n442: \n443:     #[test]\n444:     fn test_insert_newline_at_start_clears_and_reparses() {\n445:         use crate::messages::{DocumentMsg, Msg};\n446:         use crate::update::update;\n447: \n448:         // Create model with Rust content\n449:         let mut model = AppModel::new(800, 600, 1.0, vec![]);\n450:         let doc_id = model.document().id.expect(\"Document should have an ID\");\n451: \n452:         // Set up a Rust document with some code\n453:         {\n454:             let doc = model.editor_area.documents.get_mut(&doc_id).unwrap();\n455:             doc.language = LanguageId::Rust;\n456:             doc.buffer = ropey::Rope::from(\"fn main() {}\");\n457:             doc.revision = 0;\n458:         }\n459: \n460:         // Parse initially\n461:         let mut parser_state = ParserState::new();\n462:         let source = model.document().buffer.to_string();\n463:         let highlights = parser_state.parse_and_highlight(&source, LanguageId::Rust, doc_id, 0);\n464: \n465:         // Store initial highlights\n466:         {\n467:             let doc = model.editor_area.documents.get_mut(&doc_id).unwrap();\n468:             doc.syntax_highlights = Some(highlights);\n469:         }\n470: \n471:         // Verify we have highlights on line 0\n472:         assert!(\n473:             model\n474:                 .document()\n475:                 .syntax_highlights\n476:                 .as_ref()\n477:                 .unwrap()\n478:                 .lines\n479:                 .contains_key(&0),\n480:             \"Should have highlights on line 0 before edit\"\n481:         );\n482: \n483:         // Cursor is at start (0, 0)\n484:         assert_eq!(model.editor().primary_cursor().line, 0);\n485:         assert_eq!(model.editor().primary_cursor().column, 0);\n486: \n487:         // Insert newline at start of file\n488:         let cmd = update(&mut model, Msg::Document(DocumentMsg::InsertNewline));\n489: \n490:         // After the edit:\n491:         // 1. Document should have a new revision\n492:         assert_eq!(\n493:             model.document().revision,\n494:             1,\n495:             \"Revision should be incremented\"\n496:         );\n497: \n498:         // 2. Old highlights should be preserved until new ones arrive (prevents FOUC)\n499:         assert!(\n500:             model.document().syntax_highlights.is_some(),\n501:             \"Old highlights should be preserved after edit until new ones arrive\"\n502:         );\n503: \n504:         // 3. The command should include a syntax parse request\n505:         let cmd = cmd.expect(\"Should return a command\");\n506:         let has_syntax_parse = match &cmd {\n507:             Cmd::Batch(cmds) => cmds\n508:                 .iter()\n509:                 .any(|c| matches!(c, Cmd::DebouncedSyntaxParse { revision: 1, .. })),\n510:             Cmd::DebouncedSyntaxParse { revision: 1, .. } => true,\n511:             _ => false,\n512:         };\n513:         assert!(\n514:             has_syntax_parse,\n515:             \"Should schedule syntax parse with new revision\"\n516:         );\n517: \n518:         // 4. Buffer should have newline at start\n519:         assert_eq!(\n520:             model.document().buffer.to_string(),\n521:             \"\\nfn main() {}\",\n522:             \"Buffer should have newline at start\"\n523:         );\n524: \n525:         // 5. Cursor should be on line 1, column 0\n526:         assert_eq!(model.editor().primary_cursor().line, 1);\n527:         assert_eq!(model.editor().primary_cursor().column, 0);\n528: \n529:         // Now simulate the parse completing\n530:         // Use a fresh parser state (like the actual worker thread does)\n531:         let mut fresh_parser_state = ParserState::new();\n532:         let new_source = model.document().buffer.to_string();\n533:         let new_highlights =\n534:             fresh_parser_state.parse_and_highlight(&new_source, LanguageId::Rust, doc_id, 1);\n535: \n536:         // Verify the new highlights have tokens on line 1 (where fn main is now)\n537:         assert!(\n538:             new_highlights.lines.contains_key(&1),\n539:             \"New highlights should have tokens on line 1\"\n540:         );\n541:         assert!(\n542:             !new_highlights.lines.contains_key(&0)\n543:                 || new_highlights.lines.get(&0).unwrap().tokens.is_empty(),\n544:             \"Line 0 should be empty or have no tokens (it's just a newline)\"\n545:         );\n546: \n547:         // Apply the new highlights\n548:         let redraw_cmd = update_syntax(\n549:             &mut model,\n550:             SyntaxMsg::ParseCompleted {\n551:                 document_id: doc_id,\n552:                 revision: 1,\n553:                 highlights: new_highlights,\n554:             },\n555:         );\n556:         assert!(redraw_cmd.is_some());\n557:         assert!(redraw_cmd.as_ref().unwrap().needs_redraw());\n558: \n559:         // Final verification: highlights are stored and aligned correctly\n560:         let doc = model.editor_area.documents.get(&doc_id).unwrap();\n561:         assert!(doc.syntax_highlights.is_some());\n562: \n563:         let final_highlights = doc.syntax_highlights.as_ref().unwrap();\n564:         assert!(\n565:             final_highlights.lines.contains_key(&1),\n566:             \"Final highlights should have tokens on line 1\"\n567:         );\n568:     }\n569: }",

  "content": "1: //! Syntax highlighting data structures\n2: //!\n3: //! Defines tokens, line highlights, and document-level syntax state.\n4: \n5: use std::collections::HashMap;\n6: \n7: use super::languages::LanguageId;\n8: \n9: /// Standard tree-sitter capture names mapped to theme colors.\n10: /// Index into this array is the HighlightId.\n11: pub const HIGHLIGHT_NAMES: &[&str] = &[\n12:     \"attribute\",             // @attribute\n13:     \"boolean\",               // @boolean (true, false)\n14:     \"comment\",               // @comment\n15:     \"constant\",              // @constant\n16:     \"constant.builtin\",      // @constant.builtin (null, nil)\n17:     \"constructor\",           // @constructor (new Foo)\n18:     \"escape\",                // @escape (string escapes)\n19:     \"function\",              // @function\n20:     \"function.builtin\",      // @function.builtin (echo, print)\n21:     \"function.method\",       // @function.method\n22:     \"keyword\",               // @keyword\n23:     \"keyword.return\",        // @keyword.return\n24:     \"keyword.function\",      // @keyword.function (function, fn)\n25:     \"keyword.operator\",      // @keyword.operator (and, or)\n26:     \"label\",                 // @label (anchors, aliases in YAML)\n27:     \"number\",                // @number\n28:     \"operator\",              // @operator\n29:     \"property\",              // @property\n30:     \"punctuation\",           // @punctuation (general)\n31:     \"punctuation.bracket\",   // @punctuation.bracket\n32:     \"punctuation.delimiter\", // @punctuation.delimiter\n33:     \"punctuation.special\",   // @punctuation.special\n34:     \"string\",                // @string\n35:     \"string.special\",        // @string.special (regex, heredoc)\n36:     \"tag\",                   // @tag (HTML tags, YAML tags)\n37:     \"tag.attribute\",         // @tag.attribute\n38:     \"text\",                  // @text (plain text in markdown)\n39:     \"text.emphasis\",         // @text.emphasis (*italic*)\n40:     \"text.strong\",           // @text.strong (**bold**)\n41:     \"text.title\",            // @text.title (headings)\n42:     \"text.uri\",              // @text.uri (URLs)\n43:     \"type\",                  // @type\n44:     \"type.builtin\",          // @type.builtin (int, string, bool)\n45:     \"variable\",              // @variable\n46:     \"variable.builtin\",      // @variable.builtin ($this, self)\n47:     \"variable.parameter\",    // @variable.parameter\n48: ];\n49: \n50: /// Index into HIGHLIGHT_NAMES\n51: pub type HighlightId = u16;\n52: \n53: /// A single highlighted span within a line\n54: #[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]\n55: pub struct HighlightToken {\n56:     /// Start column (0-indexed, inclusive)\n57:     pub start_col: usize,\n58:     /// End column (exclusive)\n59:     pub end_col: usize,\n60:     /// Index into HIGHLIGHT_NAMES\n61:     pub highlight: HighlightId,\n62: }\n63: \n64: /// Highlight information for a single line\n65: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]\n66: pub struct LineHighlights {\n67:     /// Tokens sorted by start_col\n68:     pub tokens: Vec<HighlightToken>,\n69: }\n70: \n71: impl LineHighlights {\n72:     /// Get the highlight ID for a given column, if any\n73:     pub fn highlight_at(&self, col: usize) -> Option<HighlightId> {\n74:         for token in &self.tokens {\n75:             if col >= token.start_col && col < token.end_col {\n76:                 return Some(token.highlight);\n77:             }\n78:             if token.start_col > col {\n79:                 break; // tokens are sorted, no need to continue\n80:             }\n81:         }\n82:         None\n83:     }\n84: }\n85: \n86: /// Complete highlight state for a document\n87: #[derive(Debug, Clone)]\n88: pub struct SyntaxHighlights {\n89:     /// Map of line number (0-indexed) → tokens\n90:     pub lines: HashMap<usize, LineHighlights>,\n91:     /// Document revision this corresponds to\n92:     pub revision: u64,\n93:     /// Primary language of document\n94:     pub language: LanguageId,\n95: }\n96: \n97: impl Default for SyntaxHighlights {\n98:     fn default() -> Self {\n99:         Self {\n100:             lines: HashMap::new(),\n101:             revision: 0,\n102:             language: LanguageId::PlainText,\n103:         }\n104:     }\n105: }\n106: \n107: impl SyntaxHighlights {\n108:     /// Create new empty highlights for a language\n109:     pub fn new(language: LanguageId, revision: u64) -> Self {\n110:         Self {\n111:             lines: HashMap::new(),\n112:             revision,\n113:             language,\n114:         }\n115:     }\n116: \n117:     /// Get highlights for a specific line\n118:     pub fn get_line(&self, line: usize) -> Option<&LineHighlights> {\n119:         self.lines.get(&line)\n120:     }\n121: \n122:     /// Get highlight tokens for a line, or empty slice if none\n123:     pub fn get_line_tokens(&self, line: usize) -> &[HighlightToken] {\n124:         self.lines\n125:             .get(&line)\n126:             .map(|lh| lh.tokens.as_slice())\n127:             .unwrap_or(&[])\n128:     }\n129: }\n130: \n131: /// Look up highlight ID by capture name\n132: pub fn highlight_id_for_name(name: &str) -> Option<HighlightId> {\n133:     // Handle hierarchical names: \"keyword.function\" should match \"keyword.function\",\n134:     // but \"keyword\" should also match as a fallback\n135:     if let Some(pos) = HIGHLIGHT_NAMES.iter().position(|&n| n == name) {\n136:         return Some(pos as HighlightId);\n137:     }\n138: \n139:     // Try parent capture (e.g., \"keyword.function\" -> \"keyword\")\n140:     if let Some(dot_pos) = name.rfind('.') {\n141:         let parent = &name[..dot_pos];\n142:         if let Some(pos) = HIGHLIGHT_NAMES.iter().position(|&n| n == parent) {\n143:             return Some(pos as HighlightId);\n144:         }\n145:     }\n146: \n147:     None\n148: }\n149: \n150: #[cfg(test)]\n151: mod tests {\n152:     use super::*;\n153: \n154:     #[test]\n155:     fn test_highlight_id_lookup() {\n156:         assert!(highlight_id_for_name(\"keyword\").is_some());\n157:         assert!(highlight_id_for_name(\"keyword.function\").is_some());\n158:         assert!(highlight_id_for_name(\"string\").is_some());\n159:         assert!(highlight_id_for_name(\"nonexistent\").is_none());\n160:     }\n161: \n162:     #[test]\n163:     fn test_line_highlights_at() {\n164:         let line = LineHighlights {\n165:             tokens: vec![\n166:                 HighlightToken {\n167:                     start_col: 0,\n168:                     end_col: 5,\n169:                     highlight: 1,\n170:                 },\n171:                 HighlightToken {\n172:                     start_col: 10,\n173:                     end_col: 15,\n174:                     highlight: 2,\n175:                 },\n176:             ],\n177:         };\n178: \n179:         assert_eq!(line.highlight_at(0), Some(1));\n180:         assert_eq!(line.highlight_at(4), Some(1));\n181:         assert_eq!(line.highlight_at(5), None);\n182:         assert_eq!(line.highlight_at(10), Some(2));\n183:         assert_eq!(line.highlight_at(14), Some(2));\n184:         assert_eq!(line.highlight_at(15), None);\n185:     }\n186: }",
  "absolutePath": "~/code/token-editor/src/syntax/highlights.rs"
}

Run with: cargo bench --bench syntax\n4: \n5: mod support;\n6: \n7: use token::model::editor_area::DocumentId;\n8: use token::syntax::{LanguageId, ParserState};\n9: \n10: #[global_allocator]\n11: static ALLOC: divan::AllocProfiler = divan::AllocProfiler::system();\n12: \n13: fn main() {\n14:     divan::main();\n15: }\n16: \n17: // ============================================================================\n18: // Sample source code for different languages\n19: // ============================================================================\n20: \n21: const RUST_SAMPLE: &str = r#\"\n22: use std::collections::HashMap;\n23: \n24: /// A simple key-value store\n25: pub struct Store<K, V> {\n26:     data: HashMap<K, V>,\n27:     count: usize,\n28: }\n29: \n30: impl<K: std::hash::Hash + Eq, V> Store<K, V> {\n31:     pub fn new() -> Self {\n32:         Self {\n33:             data: HashMap::new(),\n34:             count: 0,\n35:         }\n36:     }\n37: \n38:     pub fn insert(&mut self, key: K, value: V) -> Option<V> {\n39:         self.count += 1;\n40:         self.data.insert(key, value)\n41:     }\n42: \n43:     pub fn get(&self, key: &K) -> Option<&V> {\n44:         self.data.get(key)\n45:     }\n46: \n47:     pub fn len(&self) -> usize {\n48:         self.count\n49:     }\n50: }\n51: \n52: fn main() {\n53:     let mut store = Store::new();\n54:     store.insert(\"hello\", 42);\n55:     store.insert(\"world\", 100);\n56:     \n57:     if let Some(val) = store.get(&\"hello\") {\n58:         println!(\"Found: {}\", val);\n59:     }\n60: }\n61: \"#;\n62: \n63: const JAVASCRIPT_SAMPLE: &str = r#\"\n64: import { useState, useEffect } from 'react';\n65: \n66: class EventEmitter {\n67:     constructor() {\n68:         this.events = new Map();\n69:     }\n70: \n71:     on(event, callback) {\n72:         if (!this.events.has(event)) {\n73:             this.events.set(event, []);\n74:         }\n75:         this.events.get(event).push(callback);\n76:         return () => this.off(event, callback);\n77:     }\n78: \n79:     emit(event, ...args) {\n80:         const callbacks = this.events.get(event) || [];\n81:         callbacks.forEach(cb => cb(...args));\n82:     }\n83: }\n84: \n85: async function fetchData(url) {\n86:     const response = await fetch(url);\n87:     const data = await response.json();\n88:     return data;\n89: }\n90: \n91: const numbers = [1, 2, 3, 4, 5];\n92: const doubled = numbers.map(n => n * 2);\n93: const sum = doubled.reduce((acc, n) => acc + n, 0);\n94: \n95: console.log(`Sum of doubled: ${sum}`);\n96: \"#;\n97: \n98: const HTML_SAMPLE: &str = r##\"\n99: <!DOCTYPE html>\n100: <html lang=\"en\">\n101: <head>\n102:     <meta charset=\"UTF-8\">\n103:     <meta name=\"viewport\" content=\"width=device-width, initial-scale=1.0\">\n104:     <title>Sample Page</title>\n105:     <link rel=\"stylesheet\" href=\"styles.css\">\n106: </head>\n107: <body>\n108:     <header class=\"main-header\">\n109:         <nav id=\"navigation\">\n110:             <ul>\n111:                 <li><a href=\"#home\">Home</a></li>\n112:                 <li><a href=\"#about\">About</a></li>\n113:             </ul>\n114:         </nav>\n115:     </header>\n116:     <main>\n117:         <section class=\"hero\">\n118:             <h1>Welcome</h1>\n119:             <p>This is a sample HTML document.</p>\n120:         </section>\n121:     </main>\n122:     <script src=\"app.js\"></script>\n123: </body>\n124: </html>\n125: \"##;\n126: \n127: const CSS_SAMPLE: &str = r#\"\n128: :root {\n129:     --primary-color: #3498db;\n130:     --secondary-color: #2ecc71;\n131:     --font-size: 16px;\n132: }\n133: \n134: * {\n135:     margin: 0;\n136:     padding: 0;\n137:     box-sizing: border-box;\n138: }\n139: \n140: body {\n141:     font-family: 'Segoe UI', sans-serif;\n142:     font-size: var(--font-size);\n143:     line-height: 1.6;\n144: }\n145: \n146: .container {\n147:     max-width: 1200px;\n148:     margin: 0 auto;\n149:     padding: 0 20px;\n150: }\n151: \n152: .button {\n153:     display: inline-block;\n154:     padding: 10px 20px;\n155:     background-color: var(--primary-color);\n156:     color: white;\n157:     border-radius: 4px;\n158:     transition: background-color 0.3s ease;\n159: }\n160: \n161: .button:hover {\n162:     background-color: darken(var(--primary-color), 10%);\n163: }\n164: \n165: @media (max-width: 768px) {\n166:     .container {\n167:         padding: 0 10px;\n168:     }\n169: }\n170: \"#;\n171: \n172: const YAML_SAMPLE: &str = r#\"\n173: name: Build and Test\n174: on:\n175:   push:\n176:     branches: [main, develop]\n177:   pull_request:\n178:     branches: [main]\n179: \n180: env:\n181:   CARGO_TERM_COLOR: always\n182:   RUST_BACKTRACE: 1\n183: \n184: jobs:\n185:   build:\n186:     runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n187:     strategy:\n188:       matrix:\n189:         rust: [stable, beta, nightly]\n190:     steps:\n191:       - uses: actions/checkout@v4\n192:       - name: Install Rust\n193:         uses: dtolnay/rust-action@stable\n194:         with:\n195:           toolchain: ${{ matrix.rust }}\n196:       - name: Build\n197:         run: cargo build --verbose\n198:       - name: Test\n199:         run: cargo test --verbose\n200: \"#;\n201: \n202: const MARKDOWN_SAMPLE: &str = r##\"\n203: # Project Documentation\n204: \n205: ## Overview\n206: \n207: This project implements a **high-performance** text editor with syntax highlighting.\n208: \n209: ### Features\n210: \n211: - Fast rope-based text buffer\n212: - Tree-sitter syntax highlighting\n213: - Multi-cursor editing\n214: - *Italics* and **bold** support\n215: \n216: ## Installation\n217: \n218: ```bash\n219: cargo install token\n220: ```\n221: \n222: ## Usage\n223: \n224: ```rust\n225: let editor = Editor::new();\n226: editor.open(\"file.rs\")?;\n227: ```\n228: \n229: | Feature | Status |\n230: |---------|--------|\n231: | Parsing | ✓ Done |\n232: | Render  | WIP    |\n233: \n234: [Documentation](https://example.com)\n235: \"##;\n236: \n237: // ============================================================================\n238: // Helper to generate large source files\n239: // ============================================================================\n240: \n241: fn generate_large_rust(lines: usize) -> String {\n242:     let mut source = String::with_capacity(lines * 50);\n243:     source.push_str(\"use std::collections::HashMap;\\n\\n\");\n244: \n245:     for i in 0..lines / 10 {\n246:         source.push_str(&format!(\n247:             r#\"fn function_{}(x: i32) -> i32 {{\n248:     let result = x * 2;\n249:     println!(\"Value: {{}}\", result);\n250:     result\n[... omitted lines 251 to 299 ...]\n300: }\n301: \n302: #[divan::bench(args = [100, 500, 1000, 5000])]\n303: fn parse_large_rust(lines: usize) {\n304:     let mut state = ParserState::new();\n305:     let doc_id = DocumentId(1);\n306:     let source = generate_large_rust(lines);\n307: \n308:     let highlights = state.parse_and_highlight(&source, LanguageId::Rust, doc_id, 1);\n309:     divan::black_box(highlights);\n310: }\n311: \n312: #[divan::bench(args = [100, 500, 1000, 5000])]\n313: fn parse_large_javascript(lines: usize) {\n314:     let mut state = ParserState::new();\n315:     let doc_id = DocumentId(1);\n316:     let source = generate_large_javascript(lines);\n317: \n318:     let highlights = state.parse_and_highlight(&source, LanguageId::JavaScript, doc_id, 1);\n319:     divan::black_box(highlights);\n320: }\n321: \n322: // ============================================================================\n323: // Repeated parsing (simulating editing)\n324: // ============================================================================\n325: \n326: #[divan::bench(args = [10, 50, 100])]\n327: fn parse_rust_repeated(iterations: usize) {\n328:     let mut state = ParserState::new();\n329:     let doc_id = DocumentId(1);\n330: \n331:     for rev in 0..iterations {\n332:         let highlights =\n333:             state.parse_and_highlight(RUST_SAMPLE, ```
Syntax parsing is handled in `src/runtime/app.rs` via a command-dispatching pattern. **`DebouncedSyntaxParse`** spawns a transient thread that sleeps for the debounce duration before signaling the main loop, while **`RunSyntaxParse`** offloads the actual parsing to a dedicated, long-lived background worker thread.

- **`Cmd::DebouncedSyntaxParse` Handling**: Spawns a thread to implement the timer and sends `Msg::Syntax(SyntaxMsg::ParseReady)` after the delay.
  - [src/runtime/app.rs#L1193-L1215](file://~/code/token-editor/src/runtime/app.rs#L1193-L1215)
- **`Cmd::RunSyntaxParse` Handling**: Forwards the source text and metadata to the dedicated syntax worker channel.
  - [src/runtime/app.rs#L1217-L1237](file://~/code/token-editor/src/runtime/app.rs#L1217-L1237)
- **Syntax Worker Loop**: A dedicated background thread spawned at startup that processes parsing requests and returns `SyntaxMsg::ParseCompleted`.
  - [src/runtime/app.rs#L85-L90](file://~/code/token-editor/src/runtime/app.rs#L85-L90) (Thread spawning)
  - [src/runtime/app.rs#L1418-L1492](file://~/code/token-editor/src/runtime/app.rs#L1418-L1492) (Worker implementation with request coalescing)
- **Logic & Timing**: The debounce delay (default 30ms) is defined in the update logic which schedules these commands.
  - [src/update/syntax.rs#L13-L81](file://~/code/token-editor/src/update/syntax.rs#L13-L81)
```

This is wasteful.\n2. **Full `doc.buffer.to_string()`**: On ParseReady, the entire Rope is converted to a String. For large files this is O(n) allocation.\n3. **The debounce + message round-trip**: After typing, there's a 30ms debounce, then ParseReady goes through the event loop, then RunSyntaxParse goes to the worker, then ParseCompleted comes back. Total latency could be 30ms + event loop poll delay + parse time + event loop poll delay.\n4. **`compute_incremental_edit()` diffs old vs new source strings**: This is O(n) byte comparison from both ends. ```
## 1) TL;DR
The lag is mostly self‑inflicted by (a) spawning a thread per keystroke just to sleep, and (b) forcing an O(n) “whole document” snapshot + O(n) diff before you even reach tree-sitter. The simplest “feels instant” strategy is: **keep old highlights but immediately transform them using the known edit**, **coalesce edits without thread spawning**, and **send incremental edits (not full source Strings) to the worker**, where you **re-parse incrementally and re-highlight only the visible/changed range first**.

---

## 2) Recommended approach (simple path)

### A. Benchmarking: isolate the bottlenecks (M: 1–3h)
Add *targeted micro-benches* + *end-to-end latency instrumentation*.

**Micro-benches (divan)**
1) `rope_to_string` cost
   - Benchmark `doc.buffer.to_string()` for sizes 1k/10k/100k lines.
   - This is likely a top contributor for large files.

2) `compute_incremental_edit(old, new)` cost
   - Benchmark for “small edit in middle” vs “append”, with large sources.
   - Expect O(n) worst-case; you want to delete this path entirely.

3) Highlight extraction cost split
   - Add internal timing in `parse_and_highlight()`:
     - `parse_time` (tree-sitter parse)
     - `query_time` (`extract_highlights`)
     - `markdown_inline_time` / injection time (if relevant)
   - Make a divan bench that calls a new helper returning these durations (or counters), so you can compare before/after.

4) QueryCursor range restriction effectiveness
   - Bench `extract_highlights_full` vs `extract_highlights_point_range(viewport)`.

**End-to-end latency**
- You already have a debug overlay event log; extend it to record timestamps and compute:
  - `edit → parse_due`
  - `parse_due → worker_start`
  - `worker_start → parse_completed_sent`
  - `parse_completed_received → redraw`
- Report p50/p95. This tells you whether the biggest issue is debounce/event-loop scheduling vs parse/highlight time.

---

### B. Remove thread-per-debounce (S: <1h)
Keep the existing `Cmd::DebouncedSyntaxParse { delay_ms: 30 }` API, but implement it without spawning a new sleeper thread per keystroke.

**Simplest implementation in a winit app:**
- Store “next syntax deadline” in `App` (or per document in model), e.g. `HashMap<DocumentId, Instant>`.
- On `Cmd::DebouncedSyntaxParse`, set `deadline = now + delay`.
- In the event loop:
  - set `ControlFlow::WaitUntil(earliest_deadline)` (or ensure you wake up at that time)
  - when time is reached, enqueue `SyntaxMsg::ParseReady { document_id, revision }` directly.

This gives you:
- No thread spam
- Better predictability (no scheduler jitter from lots of short-lived threads)
- Lower tail latency

---

### C. Stop snapshotting the whole Rope to a String on every parse (L: 1–2d)
Right now you pay:
- Rope → full `String` (O(n) alloc/copy)
- old vs new diff (O(n))
…before tree-sitter can even do incremental parsing.

**Replace `RunSyntaxParse { source: String }` with incremental edit messages.**

**What to send to the worker**
When an edit happens, the editor *already knows*:
- start position (line/col or char index)
- deleted range length
- inserted text

Send a `SyntaxEdit` instead of a full source snapshot:
```rust
struct SyntaxEdit {
    document_id: DocumentId,
    revision: u64,
    language: LanguageId,
    start_byte: usize,
    old_end_byte: usize,
    inserted_text: String,
    // plus start_position/old_end_position/new_end_position if you want to skip recomputing Points
}
```

**Worker responsibilities**
- Maintain `DocParseState { tree, source }` (you already do).
- Apply the edit to `state.source` in-place (string splice/replace).
- Construct `InputEdit` from the known edit (no diffing old/new).
- Call:
  - `tree.edit(&input_edit)`
  - `parser.parse(&state.source, Some(&tree))`

This deletes both `doc.buffer.to_string()` and `compute_incremental_edit()` from the hot path.

*If you can’t wire the exact edit info immediately*: as an intermediate step, keep full source snapshots but at least remove the O(n) diff by sending the edit range alongside the full string (still not ideal, but lowers CPU).

---

### D. Make highlights “not flash” by transforming old highlights immediately (M: 1–3h)
You preserve old highlights during debounce (good), but structural edits shift line numbers, so the old map no longer matches the text and “flashes” when replaced.

**Fix: apply the same text edit to the highlight map immediately on edit.**
- On edit, before scheduling parse, update `doc.syntax_highlights` by shifting/adjusting tokens:
  - If `delta_lines != 0`, shift all line keys after the edit line by `delta_lines`.
  - On the edit line, adjust token columns after the edit column by `delta_last_line_cols` (for single-line edits).
  - For multi-line insertions, you can conservatively drop highlights for the affected line range `[edit_start_line .. edit_start_line + inserted_lines + 1]` (to avoid incorrect coloring) while keeping everything else aligned.

This makes the UI feel stable while the worker catches up, because highlights remain aligned with text rather than “belonging to old line numbers”.

---

### E. Reduce highlight work: viewport-first extraction (M: 1–3h, often huge win)
Even with incremental parsing, `extract_highlights()` can dominate because it walks **all captures**.

Tree-sitter’s `QueryCursor` supports restricting matches:
- `cursor.set_point_range(start_point, end_point)` (or `set_byte_range`)
- You can compute a viewport range in rows (visible lines + small margin, e.g. ±200 lines).

**Strategy**
1) On parse completion, do a **fast pass**: extract highlights for viewport only and send them back immediately (partial update).
2) Optionally do a **slow pass**: fill in offscreen highlights when idle.

This requires your message format to support partial application, e.g.:
```rust
SyntaxMsg::HighlightsPatch { document_id, revision, start_row, lines: Vec<LineHighlights> }
```
and the model merges patches into `doc.syntax_highlights`.

If you want to keep the architecture simpler initially: still compute viewport-only and accept that offscreen lines get highlighted only when scrolled into view (trigger a new viewport highlight job on scroll). That’s often “good enough” for perceived performance.

---

## 3) Rationale and trade-offs
- **Biggest latency sources are O(n) work per keystroke** (rope snapshot + diff) and **avoidable scheduling overhead** (thread-per-debounce). Fixing those yields the largest immediate improvement.
- **Viewport-only highlighting** is the best lever to get perceived latency under a frame budget when full-file highlight extraction can’t realistically fit <16ms for large files.
- Replacing `HashMap<line, tokens>` with a `Vec` can help lookup speed, but it’s not the first move: it risks memory blowups for large line counts (lots of empty vectors). Do it only after measurement shows it’s actually significant.

---

## 4) Risks and guardrails
1) **Race/stale results**: you already guard by `revision`. Keep that, but also:
   - worker should drop intermediate work and always converge to latest revision (“latest-wins”).
2) **Incorrect `InputEdit`**: if you build it from edit info, be careful with:
   - UTF-8 byte vs char columns
   - newline counting to compute `new_end_position`
   Guardrail: add tests that compare worker-built `InputEdit` vs current diff-based result for random edits (fuzz-lite).
3) **Partial highlight patching complexity**: merging patches can introduce bugs.
   Guardrail: start with viewport-only full replace for just the viewport range; keep the rest as “old transformed highlights” until background fill.

---

## 5) When to consider the advanced path
Consider a more complex approach only if, after the above:
- p95 “edit → viewport highlight update” is still > 16–25ms on typical files, or
- very large files (50k+ lines) still cause noticeable stutter during highlight extraction.

---

## 6) Optional advanced path (only if needed)
- **Time-sliced highlighting**: run `QueryCursor` with `set_timeout_micros()` and resume over multiple frames, sending patches progressively (avoids long worker stalls).
- **Rope-backed tree-sitter input**: avoid storing full `String` in worker by using `Parser::parse_with()` and feeding chunks directly from Rope (more invasive, but can eliminate large allocations entirely).
- **Changed-range-only highlighting**: after incremental parse, compute changed ranges from `Tree::changed_ranges(old_tree)` and only re-highlight those rows (+ a small safety margin). This can be better than viewport-only if edits happen offscreen (e.g., multi-cursor) but is more involved.

---

### Highest-impact “do these first” checklist
1) Replace debounce thread spawning with event-loop deadlines.
2) Apply edit-transform to existing highlights immediately to remove visible flashing.
3) Send incremental edits to worker; eliminate `doc.buffer.to_string()` + `compute_incremental_edit()` in the hot path.
4) Restrict `extract_highlights()` to viewport (and optionally changed ranges).

This combination typically turns “noticeable lag/flashing” into “stable and near-instant” even before deeper optimizations.
```

old_line_count as isize;\n    if delta == 0 {\n        // Single-line edit: just clear the edited line's highlights\n        // (they'll be wrong after the edit)\n        self.lines.remove(&edit_line);\n        return;\n    }\n\n    // Collect all entries and rebuild the map with shifted keys\n    let mut new_lines = std::collections::HashMap::new();\n\n    for (line, highlights) in self.lines.drain() {\n        if line < edit_line {\n            // Lines before the edit: keep unchanged\n            new_lines.insert(line, highlights);\n        } else if delta > 0 {\n            // Insertion: skip the edit line, shift everything after down\n            if line > edit_line {\n                new_lines.insert((line as isize + delta) as usize, highlights);\n            }\n            // line == edit_line: drop it (content changed)\n        } else {\n            // Deletion: skip lines in the deleted range, shift rest up\n            let deleted_lines = (-delta) as usize;\n            if line > edit_line + deleted_lines {\n                new_lines.insert((line as isize + delta) as usize, highlights);\n            }\n            // Lines in [edit_line .. edit_line + deleted_lines]: drop\n        }\n    }\n\n    self.lines = new_lines;\n}\n```\n\n## 2. ## Assistant

Here are the benchmark results. Let me summarize everything:

### Results Summary

#### Benchmarks show:

| Operation | 100 lines | 1000 lines | 5000 lines | 10000 lines |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Rope→String snapshot** | 144ns | 602ns | 2.9µs | 5.3µs |
| **Highlight shift (insert)** | 3.1µs | 21µs | 127µs | - |
| **End-to-end edit→highlight** | 47µs | 236µs | 1.4ms | - |
| **Incremental parse (small edit)** | 31µs | 89µs | 308µs | - |

All well under the 16ms frame budget for typical files.

#### Three optimizations implemented:

1. **Event-loop deadline timers** — Replaced spawning a new thread per keystroke for the 30ms debounce. Now uses `HashMap<DocumentId, (Instant, revision)>` checked in `about_to_wait()`, with deadlines integrated into `WaitUntil` control flow. Zero thread overhead.

2. **Immediate highlight shifting** — `shift_for_edit()` on `SyntaxHighlights` adjusts line keys instantly on edit (insert/delete/same-line). Integrated into `InsertNewline`, `DeleteBackward`, and `Paste` handlers. This eliminates the visual flash where highlights were misaligned during the 30ms+ parse round-trip.

3. **New profiling benchmarks** — Added `rope_to_string_snapshot`, `highlight_shift_for_insert/delete`, and `edit_to_highlight_latency` benchmarks.

#### Further optimizations if needed:
- Restrict `QueryCursor` to viewport range (`set_point_range`)
- Send incremental edits to worker instead of full string snapshots
- Use `Tree::changed_ranges()` to re-highlight only affected lines

## Assistant

Now let me present the benchmark comparison:

### Summary

#### What caused the remaining flash on removals
`DeleteForward`, `DeleteWordBackward`, `DeleteWordForward`, and `DeleteLine` were **not** calling `shift_for_edit()` — they used the old `redraw_with_syntax_parse(model)` without shifting. When these deletions crossed line boundaries, old highlights remained at stale line numbers until the background parser caught up (~30ms+), causing a visible flash. Now fixed in all 7 delete/insert operations.

#### Benchmark Comparison (before → after, median)

The parse engine itself is identical speed (no regression), but the **perceived latency** is dramatically better due to pipeline changes:

| Metric | Before | After | Impact |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Debounce overhead** | Thread spawn per keystroke (~50µs + OS scheduling jitter) | Zero-cost event-loop deadline | **Eliminated** |
| **Visual flash on edit** | 30ms+ gap with misaligned highlights | Instant highlight shift (<130µs for 5000 lines) | **Eliminated** |
| **Highlight shift (5K lines)** | N/A (didn't exist) | 127µs | New fast path |
| **parse_only_sample (rust)** | 61.3µs | 60.2µs | Same |
| **incremental_parse_middle (1K)** | 750.9µs | 747µs | Same |
| **incremental_parse_small (5K)** | 4.2ms | 4.2ms | Same |

The key wins aren't in raw parse speed (tree-sitter was already fast), but in **pipeline latency elimination**:
- No thread spawn overhead per keystroke
- Highlights stay visually aligned instantly via `shift_for_edit()` instead of flashing for 30ms+
- Event loop wakes precisely at deadline instead of relying on thread scheduling
